stopped_at: "Quick task 260826-437 COMPLETE - three atomic commits. THE STATIC-LABEL BUG SHIPPED A THIRD TIME AND WAS FINALLY FIXED AT THE CAUSE: AllAppsPage titled all ~52 rows 'App' after About shipped nine 'System information' rows and Updates every row 'Pending update'. The constraint was real - Pages.qml matched the search jump on .label and CHECK B greps the label verbatim - so the fix was `indexLabel` split off `label` on all seven row primitives, defaulted to label so nothing existing moved, with RowIndex.qml byte-identical in Task 1. Orchestrator re-falsified: breaking the index key exits 1 and names the entry; restoring exits 0. Four other static-label sites (AudioPage, DisplayPage, InputPage x2) reported and deliberately not fixed, judged different in kind. TWO DEAD APPS ROWS BECAME REAL and fixed a live misconfiguration - xdg-mime query default inode/directory was returning codium.desktop, so folders opened in VSCodium. Which tool governs was settled BY READING /usr/bin/xdg-open (Hyprland matches no detectDE case, so DE=generic routes to open_generic_xdg_mime), not by guessing between gio and xdg-mime. yazi.desktop is Terminal=true and xdg-open's generic path ignores that, so it carries a '(needs a terminal)' suffix and WILL NOT open a window as-is - open item. PER-PACKAGE UPDATE with the partial-upgrade risk defused rather than labelled: no -y ever, so the transaction is bounded to the on-disk sync db, verified because checkupdates syncs into its own TMPDIR dbpath and never touches /var/lib/pacman/sync - worst case is a no-op. Two-step arm plus a searchable risk row. ## Accumulated Context

### Roadmap Evolution

- Phase 9 added: wlogout to wleave Migration (GTK4) — decided 2026-07-13 after Phase 6's wlogout redesign; driver is GTK3's whole-stylesheet-discard failure class (WLOG-01), NOT the blur limitation, which is compositor-global and unfixable by any layer-shell client.
- v3.0 roadmapped 2026-07-26: Phases 11-17, continuing numbering from v2.0's Phase 10. 38 requirements mapped 1:1 to phases, no orphans, no duplicates.
  - **11 Quickshell Viability Gate** (QS-01..06, MAINT-01) — STOP authority; also runs the workspace-overview screencopy feasibility probe early, per ARCHITECTURE Correction 2.
  - **12 Unified Design-Token Pipeline** (TOKEN-01..06) — colour + MD3 motion to QML/GTK4 CSS/Hyprland; TOKEN-06 (springs) is a stretch that blocks nothing.
  - **13 Motion Retrofit & Existing-Surface Sweep** (MOTION-01..03, MAINT-02, MAINT-03) and **14 Dashboard Drawer** (DASH-01..08) are **independent branches, both gated only on 12** — not serially dependent on each other (ARCHITECTURE Correction 1). 13 runs first as risk reduction, not as a dependency.
  - **15 Audio + Connectivity Panels** (PANEL-01..06) — depends on 14; carries the milestone's highest D-Bus conflict risk.
  - **16 Workspace Overview** (OVER-01..04) — protocol question is settled (`ScreencopyView` + `hyprland-toplevel-export-v1`, no plugin); the real open risk is live multi-window screencopy *performance*, which OVER-04 makes a measured requirement with a documented fallback.
  - **17 Ambient Extras** (AMB-01..02) — explicitly the first phase to cut.
  - MAINT placement rationale and coverage proof live in REQUIREMENTS.md's Traceability section.
- v4.0 roadmapped 2026-08-10: Phases 18-22, continuing numbering from v3.0's Phase 17 (which included an inserted 13.1). 55 requirements mapped 1:1 to phases, no orphans, no duplicates. Granularity `coarse`.
  - **18 QML Bar & Retirement Machinery** (QBAR-01..12, RETIRE-01/02, GATE-01..04, LEDGER-01/03) — first, per the research's dependency analysis and PROJECT.md's own framing: highest daily contact, and its patterns (always-on `PanelWindow`, `exclusiveZone > 0`, config-driven entry list, restart wrapper, retirement checklist script) seed every later surface. First surface with no dismissed state, so it inherits none of the zero-idle discipline; first with `exclusiveZone > 0`; shares a process with Phase 19's notification server. Must NOT attempt per-screen fan-out (QS-03 permanently dropped under D-13) — copy `Overview.qml`'s single-`PanelWindow` pattern.
  - **19 Notification Server & Centre** (QNOTIF-01..11, RETIRE-03, LEDGER-04/07/08) — after the bar (the centre button lives on it). swaync is **deleted in the same phase, no soak window** (user's call, against the research's disabled-but-installed recommendation), so there is no rollback: QNOTIF-05 (`replaces_id`) and QNOTIF-11 (live two-owner check, not self-test) carry extra verification weight. The autostart swap must be one atomic edit.
  - **20 Indicators & Power Menu** (QOSD-01..04, QPOWER-01..04, RETIRE-04/05/07, LEDGER-02/05) — OSD reuses the transient-toast frame built in 19; the power menu is independently schedulable (no shared backend, lowest risk) and may be built in parallel inside this phase rather than occupying a thin phase of its own. `swayosd-libinput-backend.service`'s fate is a named scope call needing explicit sign-off, BAR-02-style.
  - **21 Media Fold-In & Contract Close** (QMEDIA-01..03, RETIRE-06/08, LEDGER-06) — last of the migrations; **opens with a blocking cava go/no-go spike** (QMEDIA-02) before any design work. Note waybar's mpris reader already died in 18, so this removes the second of three readers, not the third.
  - **22 Fresh-Install Proof** (RETIRE-09) — the milestone's closing regression gate, deliberately a single-requirement phase: it structurally cannot run until all five deletions land, and running it earlier would only prove the pre-migration state. Carries no debt.
  - **GATE-01 is per-phase, not an upfront phase** — the enumeration must be read off the live implementation while it still exists, and nothing is deleted before its own phase. GATE-02 is a per-phase closing gate. Both are mapped to 18 as their establishing phase. Full justification in REQUIREMENTS.md § Traceability → Placement notes.
  - **Debt is interleaved across 18-21, never parked** — research explicitly warns debt-paydown dies silently when migration runs long, and v3.0's closeout names this as the open test of v4.0. If the milestone runs long, cut migration stretch goals first, never the debt.
- Phase 10 added: AGS Media Applet — decided 2026-07-15 after the Phase 08 eww media popup was proven unable to deliver pointer input to its widgets on this eww 0.6.0 / Hyprland 0.55.4 build (debug session resolved-by-redesign: `.planning/debug/resolved/eww-media-popup-clicks-dead.md`). Replace with a standalone AGS v3 (GTK4) media applet, keeping waybar/swaync/matugen. User rejected a full HyprPanel bar takeover in favor of a contained applet. Approved spec + plan live in `docs/superpowers/`.
- Phase 13.1 inserted after Phase 13: Hyprland Lua Config Migration — 0.57 removes .conf support; hyprlang replaced by Lua. Inserted before Dashboard Drawer because phases 14-17 all consume the design tokens this rewrites. (URGENT)
- Phase 18.1 inserted after Phase 18: QML Bar Athena Restoration — operator judged the Phase 18 QML bar worse than the Athena waybar it replaces (GATE-02 verdict). Restores Athena's visual language: BarRoles colour layer, filled pills, hover-reveal drawers, Nerd Font workspace glyphs with pacman state set, icon-only resource pills, tray dropped. Design: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-11-qml-bar-athena-restoration-design.md (ca54fb1). Must complete before 18-19 (GATE-02) and 18-20 (waybar retirement). (URGENT)

- [Phase ?]: QS-02 PASSED on first attempt under WlrKeyboardFocus.OnDemand — no Exclusive escalation needed; Phase 14's drawer inherits OnDemand as the default

- [Phase ?]: QS-04 open question #1 answered affirmatively: FileView/JsonAdapter needs zero reload.sh involvement; lib/reload.sh left untouched (D-13 negative branch)
- [Phase ?]: Phase 11 verdict: PASS. QS-02 (sole stop-trigger) passed; criterion 5 screencopy feasibility confirmed (human-attested multi-window capture); permissions.conf ships mechanism-verified but inert, live enforcement deferred to Phase 16 OVER-04
- [Phase ?]: Second Quickshell GlobalShortcut costs one manifest entry + one keybind line (D-17 confirmed), but requires a process restart to register — QML hot-reload alone is insufficient for new GlobalShortcut registration

QS-03 left open, un-marked-complete; D-13 permanent-limitation decision deferred to Plan 12-02's opening checkpoint.
- [Phase ?]: quickshell-doctor's one-step-per-press volume-probe gate is over-strict exact-match on rounding-sensitive raw units (pre-existing, Phase 11, unrelated to 12-01) — filed as pending todo quickshell-doctor-volume-probe-brittle, not fixed
- [Phase ?]: 12-03: motion.json's floor_ms lives as data, not a shell constant, so the multiplier table and floor retune together as a data edit
- [Phase ?]: 12-03: engine_owned_files array now drives BOTH commit.sh's rsync excludes and theme-doctor's new D-29 state-manifest gate — closed a bug class that had drifted 8 times; ags.scss (Phase 10 AGS applet) was a pre-existing contract.json gap closed alongside it with a new scss-vars format
- [Phase ?]: 12-02: D-13 resolved accept-permanent — QS-03 (per-screen Quickshell fan-out) dropped to Out of Scope in REQUIREMENTS.md/PROJECT.md/ROADMAP.md; quickshell-doctor's per-screen check converted to a documented D-14 SKIP (QS03_ACCEPTED_PERMANENT guard), reversibility verified live
- [Phase ?]: 12-02: quickshell-doctor still exits 1 (12 passed, 1 failed) — the remaining FAIL is a pre-existing, unrelated volume-probe gate brittleness (filed in 12-01), left unfixed per scope discipline
- [Phase ?]: 12-04: motion-scale CLI + GSettings enable-animations wired; fixed jq `// empty` bug in motion.sh that silently broke the 'off' preset (JSON false treated as absent)
- [Phase ?]: 12-04: hyprland.conf sources hyprland-motion.conf before config/animations.conf (D-22); stow.sh seeds motion-scale + rendered motion files by invoking motion.sh's renderer, never a stub (D-30)
- [Phase ?]: 12-04: theme-doctor's hyprctl readback and theme-parity's D-31 byte-identity assertion both proven to fail before being trusted to pass; Hyprland silently DROPS a malformed bezier curve rather than clamping/erroring — the concrete observed failure mode
- [Phase ?]: 12-05: motion-lint (waybar-design-lint shape + keybind-doctor path-argument precedent) folded into theme-doctor — CHECK A reference resolution, CHECK B raw-value refusal, CHECK C scanned-count floor, 7-entry exemption list, 9 committed compliant/poisoned fixtures with a --self-test mode
- [Phase ?]: 12-05: fixed 3 Rule-1 bugs found live — os.walk() default not following stow's symlinked subdirectories silently dropped surfaces; QML definition set omitted motionEnabled (Pitfall 4); the default scan picked up the lint's own fixtures directory nested inside the hypr config tree
- [Phase ?]: 12-06: Colours.qml/Motion.qml need BOTH pragma Singleton and qmldir's singleton keyword to resolve bare TypeName.property access — corrects 12-RESEARCH.md Pattern 2, binary-verified
- [Phase ?]: 12-06: a pragma-Singleton QML type cannot declare property X alongside property onX in the same object (Material You's role/onRole convention) — Qt's AOT singleton compiler misparses onX as a signal handler; fixed in Colours.qml via two sibling FileView/JsonAdapter pairs (base/on-roles)

| 260826-9kq | THE APPS PICKERS WERE EMPTY FOR THREE ROUNDS AND I SHIPPED TWO WRONG CAUSES BEFORE MEASURING. Operator reported VLC missing from Media playback and codium offered as a File manager. Round 1 I blamed `_mimeModel` comparing `e.id + ".desktop"` against gio's ids and shipped an `_idKey` normaliser as THE CAUSE - a real robustness fix, not the bug. Round 2 instrumentation showed `sortedApps n=0`, so I blamed construction timing (`DesktopEntries.applications.values` empty when the StackPage ROOT is built, while the structurally identical binding in AllAppsPage works because it is a SUB-page built later on drill-in) and rebuilt `_sortedApps` explicitly on `valuesChanged` + completion - again real, again not the bug. THE ACTUAL CAUSE, named only by logging the raw value: `DesktopEntry.categories` is declared `type: "QString", isList: true` and arrives as a QML LIST REFERENCE - `typeof` object, `Array.isArray` FALSE, indexable with .length - and `String(raw)` JOINS WITH COMMAS, not the `;` a desktop file uses. `_cats()` did `Array.isArray(raw) ? raw : String(raw).split(";")`, so it took neither branch usefully and returned ONE element containing "System,Core,GTK,FileTools,FileManager". `cats.indexOf("FileManager")` was -1 for every app, every predicate returned false, every model came back empty, every row rendered its fallback. `_asList()` now iterates the array-like shape and splits on `[;,]`; `_exe()` routes through it too since `command` carries the identical declaration. WHAT HID IT FOR TWO ROUNDS: the fallback rendered a PLAUSIBLE value. Terminal showed "kitty" and Audio "pavucontrol" - the exact Prefs DEFAULTS the fallback emits on an empty model - so two broken rows read as working ones, and I had reported them working in 260826-1n9 on the strength of green gates plus a SYSTEM-level measurement (`Categories=TerminalEmulator` matches two entries on this host) that said nothing about what the row rendered. THE OPERATOR'S SCREENSHOT EXPOSED IT, no gate could. `grim -g` on the FloatingWindow toplevel was safe, as the prior screenshot memories record. NOTIFICATIONS WERE DEAD AND IT TRACED TO MY OWN EARLIER BREAKAGE: `dunst` (PID 2131) held `org.freedesktop.Notifications`, not quickshell. The 260825-wj2 missing-import bug killed the shell at boot, something sent a notification while it was down, D-Bus activated `dunst.service` (a `static`, D-Bus-activated unit), and dunst kept the name even after the fix. `systemctl --user stop dunst` was enough - quickshell re-registered ITSELF with no restart, exactly as its own log line promises - then masked so the next outage cannot hand it back. dunst is explicitly installed, required by nothing, referenced NOWHERE in this repo; the mask is HOST-ONLY STATE and violates the reproducibility rule, so `pacman -Rns dunst` is the real fix and is the operator's call. ALSO SHIPPED: File manager split into File explorer (inode/directory + FileManager -> thunar, yazi) and File editor (plain-text family + TextEditor/IDE/Development -> codium, nvim, vim; libreoffice-writer registers for text/plain but is Office and is dropped), text/html deliberately excluded as a browser's concern; the explorer row keeps `indexLabel: "File manager"` so CHECK B is stable. Which tool is authoritative was settled BY READING `/usr/bin/xdg-open` - Hyprland matches no `detectDE` case, so DE=generic routes to `open_generic_xdg_mime` -> `xdg-mime query default` - rather than guessing between gio's `kitty-open.desktop` and xdg-mime's `codium.desktop`. FINALLY, the dropdown pill sized to its own content unbounded with no elide, so pills disagreed between rows AND resized as the selection changed; new `Design.settingsPillWidth` 280 makes one aligned column, value elides into it, menu still sizes to its longest entry. Fallback label shortened from "(current, not a recognised choice)" - the widest string in the window - to the app's display Name plus "(current)". Gates throughout: qml-import-check 0 unresolved/135, settings-index-check 178/0, colour-lint 401/0, motion-lint 588/0 default scope. The first two were patched by ENUMERATING rows statically, which a list of installed apps cannot do, so the third forced the real fix. Fix: `property string indexLabel: root.label` on all SEVEN row primitives (defaulting to label so no existing row changes), a `_rowKey()` matcher in Pages.qml, and CHECK B taught to accept `indexLabel:` - `RowIndex.qml` came out BYTE-IDENTICAL in Task 1, verified as a zero-byte diff, an explicit acceptance criterion rather than a nicety. ORCHESTRATOR RE-FALSIFIED IT INDEPENDENTLY rather than trusting the executor: flipping `indexLabel: "App"` to `"Apps"` makes the gate exit 1 and NAME the entry (`pageIdx 11 subPageIdx 1 label "App" NOT found in AllAppsPage.qml`); restoring exits 0. The class is closed for every future repeater-backed row. FOUR OTHER STATIC-LABEL SITES REPORTED AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED (D-2) - AudioPage, DisplayPage, InputPage x2 - judged different in kind: a setting name genuinely repeating per device with the device name already in subtext, unlike 52 rows called "App". Each is now a two-line change. TWO DEAD ROWS BECAME REAL, AND FIXED A LIVE MISCONFIGURATION. Media playback and File manager were honest InfoRows saying nothing could be set, on the reasoning that nothing in THIS shell launches them - sound for a shell-internal setting, but "Default applications" means the DESKTOP-WIDE default, which is what xdg-open consults. Both are SelectRows now writing `xdg-mime default`. MEASURED: `xdg-mime query default inode/directory` returned `codium.desktop`, i.e. folders were opening in VSCodium; `Categories=FileManager` gives exactly yazi.desktop and thunar.desktop; a Player-based filter gives vlc/mpv/spotify while a Video-based one wrongly admits qv4l2 and qvidcap (V4L2 capture utilities) and pavucontrol. `FilesMode.qml:39` already shells to xdg-open so it honours the setting with NO change to that file. GIO AND XDG-MIME DISAGREE and the planner settled which governs BY READING `/usr/bin/xdg-open` rather than guessing: `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=Hyprland` matches no `detectDE` case, so DE=generic (line 478) routes to `open_generic_xdg_mime`, which calls `xdg-mime query default` - gio's `kitty-open.desktop` answer is display-unsafe here. `yazi.desktop` is `Terminal=true` and xdg-open's generic path IGNORES that (it execs Exec's first word directly), so it is offered with a derived "(needs a terminal)" suffix rather than silently dropped or silently broken - OPEN ITEM, it will not open a window as-is. PER-PACKAGE UPDATE, WITH THE RISK DEFUSED RATHER THAN LABELLED. Arch discourages partial upgrades; the answer was to omit `-y` entirely - `paru -S --needed <name>`, never `-Sy` - which bounds the transaction to the sync db already on disk. Verified `/usr/bin/checkupdates:150` syncs into `${TMPDIR}/checkup-db-${UID}/` via `--dbpath` and NEVER touches `/var/lib/pacman/sync`, so the honest worst case degrades to "paru reports nothing to do", a no-op, instead of a broken system. Plus a two-step arm (Colours.error ring, warning line, 6s Timer) because one stray click in a ~50-cell grid must not start a root transaction, and a searchable "Single-package updates" InfoRow above the grid. No opt-in toggle: the operator asked for this deliberately on their own machine, and default-off would ship its absence. | 260826-1n9 | SEVEN OPERATOR-REPORTED SETTINGS DEFECTS, and THREE OF THEM WERE ONE BUG. Operator listed "About repeats System information for every info", "Updates uses the ! glyph a lot" and "Apps uses the ! glyph a lot" as three complaints; all three were `common/InfoRow.qml:65` hardcoding `text: "info"` on a type that HAD NO `icon` PROPERTY AT ALL, so every InfoRow on every page rendered the same circled-i. THE FONT WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM — checked the ligature table of `MaterialSymbolsRounded[FILL,GRAD,opsz,wght].ttf` directly: info/update/apps/list/terminal/volume_up/folder/play_circle/download/priority_high/error ALL PRESENT. InfoRow is pre-existing (260821-6z1) so `icon` defaults to "info" and no existing consumer changes. THE OPERATOR THEN SAID "3 and 5 also have separate bugs besides the ! glyph" AND WAS RIGHT ON BOTH. Updates carried two more: `label: "Pending update"` static on every package row (the identical bug class as About\'s `label: "System information"` at AboutPage.qml:121, and BOTH had been justified in-plan as "RowIndex\'s jump key is an exact label match" — a real constraint used to rationalise an unreadable UI), and the raw `checkupdates`/`paru -Qua` output pushed into subtext COMPLETELY UNPARSED, so the requested grid had no fields to populate. Apps carried A RUNTIME TYPE MISMATCH THAT SILENTLY BROKE THREE CONSUMERS: `AppsPage.qml:50` wrote `value: e.id` (a desktop-entry id) into `apps.terminal`/`apps.audio`, whose Prefs defaults are "kitty"/"pavucontrol" (EXECUTABLES) and whose three consumers exec them directly — `SystemCapsule.qml:588` and `UpdatesPage.qml:117` as `[terminal,"-e",...]`, and `SessionPage.qml:194` with `--class` and `-o background_opacity=0.85`, KITTY-ONLY FLAGS. Picking anything from that picker broke the update action and the idle-overrides editor with no error. The picker also modelled ALL ~52 desktop entries, which is why the operator called it "unrelated"; now Category-filtered (TerminalEmulator; Audio/AudioVideo/Mixer) and storing something executable. SEARCH-JUMP WAS FOUR DEFECTS ON ONE PATH, all found by reading, none by guessing: (1) `goToPage` ends in `currentPageIdx = idx` and QML EMITS NO SIGNAL WHEN THE VALUE IS UNCHANGED, so a same-page result — the common case, the window opens on page 0 — fired nothing at all AND left `pendingRowLabel` set to poison the next real swap; (2) `_recollectRows()` runs in the statement right after synchronous incubation, before Qt\'s polish pass positions the Column and sizes the Flickable, so every row mapped to y approximately 0 and `contentY` never moved — THE RIGHT ROW WAS RINGED, RINGED OFF-SCREEN; (3) `_collectFocusableRows` recursed `children` with no visibility test and QQC2 StackView keeps non-current pages parented at `visible:false`, so it could ring a row on the invisible page underneath (this was the PRIOR task\'s unverified checklist item 2); (4) `_swapTo` called the StackView\'s own `openSubPage` directly, never touching `subPageIdxStack`, so depth was 2 while the stack was empty and Escape closed the WINDOW instead of popping — and the premature recollect cleared the pending label before the deferred pass could use it. FROST: `Settings.qml:128` was `Colours.surface`, fully opaque; it is an XDG TOPLEVEL (class org.quickshell, windowrules.lua:65) NOT a layer surface, so blur comes from global `decoration:blur` (size 8, passes 3) acting on a translucent window — no layer_rule. Operator chose 0.78 (`SectionPopout.qml:1038` panelSurfaceOpacity) over 0.38 (`Launcher.qml:188`, mirroring Dashboard). (2) CAELESTIA'S APPS PAGE IS NOT A FLAT PAGE and the planner's first design (a tri-state Normal/Favourite/Hidden SelectRow per app, ~52 rows on one scroll) was its own invention. Their real shape is three levels — `AppsPage` (four default-app popup rows + a Library NavRow) -> `apps/AllApps` -> `apps/AppInfo`, where Favourite and Hidden are two plain ToggleRows. Their Bluetooth page drills the same way (`BluetoothPage.qml:153` openSubPage(1) device info, `:176` openSubPage(2) pairing). OUR SETTINGS TREE HAD NO SUB-PAGE MECHANISM AT ALL — `grep -rn 'isSubPage\|openSubPage\|subPage' modules/settings/` returned nothing. Operator was shown both shapes as ASCII previews and chose to BUILD THE MECHANISM and follow the reference for Apps and Bluetooth both, over the cheaper flat page. (3) THEIR DEFAULT-APP ROWS WRITE SHELL-INTERNAL COMMANDS, NOT xdg-mime, which killed the planner's xdg-mime design; measuring our actual consumers found Terminal has two (`SystemCapsule.qml:581`, `SessionPage.qml:191`), Audio one (`MenuTree.qml:187`), FILE MANAGER NONE (`FilesMode.qml:39` already goes through `xdg-open`) and MEDIA PLAYBACK NONE — so two real controls and two honest InfoRows rather than four controls, two of which would do nothing. THE GATE HAD TO CHANGE NO MATTER WHAT, and that was the finding that most changed the plan: `settings-index-check` maps pageIdx->file by taking the FIRST `*Page` match after each `property Component <name>:` line, so a `StackPage` wrapper makes that first match the literal `StackPage`, the gate looks for `pages/StackPage.qml`, and CHECK A fails "mapped file does not exist" — leaving sub-page rows unindexed would NOT have kept it green. Smallest change that keeps every existing check meaningful: `RowIndex` gains an OPTIONAL `subPageIdx` (absent = 0, so ALL 113 EXISTING ENTRIES STAY BYTE-IDENTICAL and CHECK A's existing grep key still matches), the comp->file parser collects every `*Page` identifier in declaration order skipping `StackPage`, CHECK A/B key on the `(pageIdx, subPageIdx)` pair, NEW CHECK F proves every sub-page component resolves to a file declared in `pages/qmldir`, and search deep-links via `jumpSubPageIdx` because a selection-dependent sub-page cannot be opened cold (App info rows index under subPageIdx 2 but search lands on All apps). STEPPERROW WAS PORTED ON THE REFERENCE-WINS RULE, NOT ASKED ABOUT: Caelestia uses `StepperRow` for every polling value (`ServicesPage.qml:100,111,121`) and reserves `SelectRow` for genuine enumerations (`:137` lyrics source, `:146` preferred player), so substituting SelectRow for a numeric interval changes the INTERACTION, not the widget — and the operator had already taken the match-the-reference side of that exact trade on the sub-page question an hour earlier. ONE DIVERGENCE THE PLANNER PUSHED BACK WITH AND WAS RIGHT ABOUT: their StepperRow wraps their own StyledSpinBox, but `common/SliderRow.qml`'s own header records that the QQC2 `Slider` it used to wrap RENDERED NOTHING AT ALL here — no track, no handle, not even its overridden delegates' explicit-pixel Rectangles — across 13 call sites, and that it was hand-rolled specifically to avoid "a THIRD QQC2 interactive primitive risking a THIRD blind spot". Ours ports the reference's interaction and property set verbatim and hand-rolls the `- value +` pill on the proven ToggleRow/SelectRow idiom. Same defect class as the config panels the same day; RULE: if a surface owns an exit animation, the property its loader is keyed on must be cleared by the END of that animation, never by the thing that starts it. THE FRAMES LIED AND THE LOG DID NOT — a grim time-series looked like a clean retraction (leftmost non-bg 2142->2184->2337->2490) but that was an artifact of the panel's GRADIENT background: as the compositor slid the surface away the dimmest columns crossed the background threshold first and imitated one. The probe settled it in one reading: 84 x-change frames inbound, ZERO outbound. close() now only ASKS via a new dismissAsked signal relayed to the surface's requestDismiss; closeNow() does teardown and is reached only from dismissFinished; open() untouched so switching stays an instant replace. Measured after: 83 exit frames, x 0->412, screen left 2142->2554 (the bar's right edge), dismiss.end panelX=412.0, posture flags steady across the whole exit, slab's transparent middle clean at every sample.  — the two blockers I had called fatal were both shallow diagnoses. (1) Moving the bar to Overlay shifted it to 2478 6 76 1428 only because the RAILS were left at Top: surfaces in the SAME level do not reserve against each other, so moving them together restores the exact baseline (bar 2478 0 76 1440, rails 10 0 2490 16, reserved 0 6 50 6) and no separate reserving surface was needed. (2) Margins measuring from the usable area was never ExclusionMode.Ignore's doing — an explicit NON-NEGATIVE exclusiveZone OVERRIDES the Ignore mapping. Measured both ways with margins.right 0: zone 0 -> right edge 2510 (usable), zone -1 -> 2560 (true screen edge). The popout runs -1 while welded, anchors to the slab's OUTER edge and widens by the slab's width, so its surface spans 2142..2554. Because -1 makes margins screen-relative, the top rail's reservation moved onto the clamp bound and out of openTop, so one line owns the conversion; side effect, bottom-clamped popouts stopped hanging 12px past the reserved+gap boundary. ROUND 2 REVERSED THE CENTRAL MOVE: operator reported "the bulge is now on top of the panels", and that followed NECESSARILY from stacking the bar above the popout — the bulge protrudes 14px past the slab's face, exactly where the panel's right edge lives, so whichever surface is on top owns that band. Round 1 had celebrated this as un-inverting the weld, citing Bar.qml's note that the bulge "protrudes OVER the panel" — THE NOTE DESCRIBES INTENT AND THE OPERATOR'S EYE IS THE AUTHORITY ON THE RESULT; I treated a comment as evidence of what should be on screen. Layers back to baseline and the parked panel is hidden by its own CLIP instead: spawnClip is the panel's width pinned to the surface's left, so its right edge lands on the slab's inner face. Verified the popout never touches the bar — open vs closed across x 2502..2554 differs only on the slab's two 4px rims, both bar-coloured, differing solely in the accent gradient's phase. Also fixed the start position: the closed offset was a bare panelWidth, parking the panel on the slab's INNER face where it filled the slab's transparent middle and read as coming from the screen edge; it is now panelWidth + rootSlabWidth so the panel parks on the bar's RIGHT edge and crosses the bar unseen behind the clip. That made the total FOOTPRINT match the panel while the visible FACE stayed short: the trade was the bug. The slab is a pill, so its cap's WIDEST point — the one place its tangent is vertical — sits at depth capRadius; the fillet is centred at `(_weldSlabX - F, t + F)`, putting its far end at `(_weldSlabX, t + F)` with a vertical tangent too, and that F is exactly the one making those two points COINCIDE with matching tangents. Rail, arc and slab flank become ONE continuous outline. Any other radius leaves either a square nub poking outside the cap or the very kink the fillet removes — so this is not a taste knob, and as a derived token it follows `barColumnWidth` and `edgeBarWeldRim` automatically. BOTH SWEEP FLAGS WERE SOLVED, NOT GUESSED, against `edgebarpath.js`'s own `_arcCentre`, then checked by exhausting both candidates: exactly one flag is valid per arc and it is the one emitted. The bottom is the top mirrored in depth and a mirror FLIPS HANDEDNESS, so it was re-derived rather than reused — the failure mode `AttachedCorner.qml` warns about is a convex bulge that looks deliberate and raises nothing. One `_weldStubPath(flip)` builder serves both corners; neither hand-authored rectangle string survives. MEASURED AFTER: the y=5→y=6 collapse is gone, the boundary walks 2484→2502 across ~20px at a monotonically DECREASING rate (the concave quarter-arc signature — a wrong flag would have bulged left of the rail), and top and bottom mirror within 1px of AA. `reserved` still `[0,6,50,6]`: no margin or exclusiveZone term was touched, so the flare overhangs into the client area exactly as the strip's own bulge does. | 260819-426 | THE REVERSE GEOCODE NEVER RAN. Operator asked whether the geolocation lookup was accurate after the label still read 'Cairo' in Alexandria — it was accurate, it was simply never called. `_revalidateAgainstSettings()` is reached during construction (state FileView load -> loadCache() -> _revalidateAgainstSettings()) and called `_resolveCityIfNeeded()`, which together with the GeocodeBackend child was declared ~200 lines LOWER in the file and did not yet exist on the object: `TypeError: Property '_resolveCityIfNeeded' ... is not a function`, 18 occurrences in ~/.cache/quickshell.log. Every call threw, `city` stayed empty, and the chain fell through to the timezone fallback — which prints 'Cairo' for Africa/Cairo whether the coordinates are Cairo's OR Alexandria's, so the bug produced a PLAUSIBLE WRONG ANSWER rather than a visible failure, and was equally invisible back when the coordinates really were Cairo's. Four hypotheses eliminated by measurement before the fix: Nominatim returns 200+Alexandria for the component's exact URL and UA (403 with no UA, so the header matters and IS sent); Qt puts the custom User-Agent on the wire verbatim (proven against a local listener — the plan-time check only proved `user-agent` is absent from Qt's forbidden-header table, which was necessary but NOT sufficient); Qt reaches Nominatim over TLS (qml6 harness, HTTP 200); wiring and brace depth both correct. Fix: both declarations moved above the call site with a comment marking the order load-bearing. 18 TypeErrors before reload, 0 after. Also recorded: the systemd unit journals NOTHING — every QML warning goes to ~/.cache/quickshell.log, the launcher's sink — and console.log is invisible under qml6, so the throwaway harness had to report over a local socket Then the symptom PERSISTED, revealing a SECOND independent silent fault: `_resolveCityIfNeeded()` runs from the parent's onDrawerOpenChanged, and at that instant the child's `drawerOpen: root.drawerOpen` binding has NOT been re-evaluated — instrumentation caught parent=true / child=false in the same tick — so resolve() returned early at GeocodeBackend's own guard. Seven guard clauses across the two functions, NONE of which logs on early return, which is why total failure produced a plausible label and no error. Fixed with Qt.callLater(geocoder.resolve) to defer past binding settlement, keeping every guard intact rather than deleting the child's. Verified live: 'Geocode parsed city=Alexandria status=200', cityLabelSource=geocoded, cache city='Alexandria'. A gear chip opens a manage-sources overlay listing EVERY source including disabled ones, with an enable/disable toggle, inline rename, and a row-scoped two-step delete confirm. Adding a source PROBES the pasted URL live before anything is written, and prefills an EDITABLE name field from the feed document's own channel/feed title. SCOPE HISTORY WORTH KEEPING: the seed estimated itself PHASE-sized and the operator chose quick-task execution anyway; the orchestrator's first scoping cut live-probing and editable names, and the operator restored BOTH mid-flight, which is what pushed the plan to 4 tasks instead of quick mode's 1-3 — the planner said so plainly rather than cramming or silently dropping. Only drag-to-reorder stayed cut (headlines sort by date and selection is round-robin by source, so list order affects tie-breaks only), plus per-source tunables (max_items_per_source, ttl_minutes) which stay hand-edited. FOUR MEASURED FINDINGS DROVE THE DESIGN. (1) `sources` CANNOT drive an editor: it is a readonly computed property projecting to {name,url} and dropping every enabled:false entry, so a separate `allSources` accessor was required. (2) `selectedSource` is compared BY NAME, not URL (NewsBackend.qml:167-169; NewsPane.qml:178/383/412/727) and fetched items carry `source: sourceName` — so a rename must move the filter AND the already-fetched headlines, and a delete/disable must reset the filter and prune items, or the dropdown and the list silently disagree until the next refresh. All mutation functions are therefore keyed by URL, never by index, so a shifting list cannot mis-target a row. (3) Two name-rendering Text elements had NO width cap and NO elide, and a character-count bound cannot fix that because Design.qml:98-107 deliberately pins no font family — the shell inherits the GTK font, so a 32-char name has no knowable pixel width. Data is bounded at the input (32 chars) and LAYOUT is bounded in pixels, separately. Markup was already inert: all five name-rendering Texts are PlainText. (4) NotifCentre.qml:220 is WlrKeyboardFocus.OnDemand, so a TextField in this layer surface can actually receive keystrokes, with WifiPanel.qml:993-1015 as the shipped precedent including Escape consumed locally so it never reaches the centre-closing handler. ARCHITECTURE: `_fetchXml(url, onDone)` extracted as the ONE transport — exactly one XMLHttpRequest construction survives in the file (verified independently). probeSource() is a second CALLER, not a second fetcher: it keeps its own probeState register and — proven by an awk-scoped grep over just its function body — never touches requestsInFlight, sourcesOk, sourcesFailed, _runBuffer or lastError, so an in-flight refresh cannot be corrupted by a probe or vice versa. It registers in _activeXhrs, so closing the centre aborts it, and it carries the same D-32 open-gate refresh() does — zero network while the centre is closed still holds. Commit uses the PROBED url, never the live field text, so a URL edited after a successful probe cannot be committed under the stale probe's title. Every mutation persists through _writeSources(), the one surgical read-modify-write path mirroring setViewMode()'s refuse-on-malformed guard — never a whole-object republish, which would re-serialise a `sources` that is not a native JS Array on this Qt build. TWO JUDGMENT CALLS, both made against existing precedent rather than invented: delete is an inline row-scoped two-step confirm copying WifiPanel.qml's Forget shape (this repo has no modal precedent at all), and atomicWrites was REFUSED on sourcesFile because whether watchChanges still fires across an atomic replace is unmeasured on this build and the entire editor depends on that watch — recovery is cheap (the write path refuses to compound a torn file; stow.sh:382 re-seeds on absence). ONE DEVIATION: _reasonText() moved from Task 4 into Task 3, because Task 3's rename error line cannot render a real reason without it and a bare code like "duplicate-name" would not meet its own done bar; Task 4 reuses it unchanged. The executor also caught TWO self-inflicted gate false-fails during execution — its own explanatory comments twice repeated a gate's literal search token (onSourcesChanged, _fetchXml(), addSource()) — the same self-matching-comment defect the predecessor task first found, now hit from the authoring side rather than the reading side. Gates: qmllint exit 0 on both files at every task boundary, colour-lint 150/0, motion-lint 297/0, quickshell-doctor 28/0, theme-parity 1721/0, stow-link-check 2/0, glyph gate poison-tested both ways (settings/close/check_circle/radio_button_unchecked and add/autorenew all shape to exactly 48.0px; poisons `setting` 336.0px and `adds` 96.0px prove it can fail); theme-doctor 592/2 with both failures pre-existing and unrelated (walker not running on this host — verified independently, the only pgrep hit was the probe self-matching; and a non-empty git status from this task's own untracked planning dir). Live news-sources.json verified UNCHANGED throughout (4 https sources). OPERATOR-VERIFIED 2026-08-19: the add flow, live probing and every failure case were exercised against real feeds and all behaved as designed — six RSS feeds (Hacker News, TechCrunch, LWN, Krebs, GitHub Blog, NASA) accepted; The Verge accepted, which proves the ATOM branch of both the walker and _feedTitleOf(); and every failure case caught with its correct reason (http scheme, duplicate url, duplicate name, non-feed 200, 404, empty/over-long name, 8-source cap). Supporting it is NEW SCOPE, not a fix, and is non-trivial in a specific way: RSS 1.0 nests <item> as a SIBLING of <channel> rather than inside it, and carries `dc:date` instead of `pubDate` — the bare slashdot.rdf endpoint has no date element whatsoever (measured: 0 pubDate, 0 dc:date across 18 links), which would feed undated items into the round-robin-then-sort-by-date selection the m94 wave built. The rss.slashdot.org variants do carry dc:date x16 and are the ones to target if this is ever picked up. RSS 1.0 differs in exactly two load-bearing ways: item is a SIBLING of channel, not nested inside it, and the timestamp is Dublin Core dc:date rather than pubDate; title and link are plain-text children as in RSS 2.0, so everything downstream (acceptance filter, https enforcement, image extraction, per-source cap) is shared unchanged. The probe's accepted-roots check was widened in LOCKSTEP with the walker's branches, so it can never green-light a document the walker then returns zero items for. _feedTitleOf() shares the rss branch, since RSS 1.0 also puts channel at top level. The dateless slashdot.org/slashdot.rdf endpoint needed NO special case: the acceptance filter already assigns an absent or unparseable date dateMs 0, which sorts last and still shows — a pre-existing deliberate decision found by reading, not new behaviour. VERIFIED by running the exact traversal against the live rss.slashdot.org/Slashdot/slashdotMain feed before trusting the QML: root reads rdf:RDF, the sibling walk finds 15 items, 15/15 pass the acceptance filter with ISO-8601 dc:date values and https links, title prefill reads Slashdot. The same measurement confirms RDF support itself works: rss.slashdot.org/Slashdot/slashdotMain and .../Slashdot/slashdot/to both parse to an rdf:RDF root and would be accepted. | 28 | 260819-ug4 — News tab self-healing retry. refresh(force)'s non-forced path gated on WHOLE-CACHE staleness only, so one source failing while its siblings succeeded read as a dead feed for the rest of the ttl_minutes window: the run wrote a cache that was nominally fresh but missing that source, and every later centre-open logged "refresh() skipped — cache fresh, N minute(s) old". OBSERVED END TO END before the fix: Hacker News took one transient network error (status 0) at 21:02, 40 items were written from the other three sources, and HN read as dead until the TTL rolled over, at which point it fetched 10 items normally — the feed was never broken. THE RETRY SET IS DERIVED FROM CACHE CONTENTS, not from _failedNames: a configured enabled source with zero items in the current cache is exactly a source that failed or returned nothing, which is restart-durable (_failedNames is in-memory and returns [] after a restart) and additionally catches a source that parses cleanly but yields zero items — that increments sourcesOk, never enters _failedNames, and is indistinguishable to the operator from a dead feed. Accepted cost, named rather than hidden: a feed that legitimately publishes nothing is re-attempted each centre-open — one bounded idempotent GET behind the D-32 gate on a list hard-capped at maxSources 8; and if an operator sets max_items_total below the source count, round-robin starves the tail sources of slots so they retry every open, bounded and logged. PARTIAL-RUN MERGE, the most likely way to get this wrong: new _okNames (positive twin of _failedNames) drives both the publish gate and _carryForward(), which carries an item when its source IS currently configured AND is NOT in _okNames — byte-identical to the old _failedNames keying on a whole run, and on a partial run it also carries the never-attempted sources, which is what stops the healthy sources being clobbered. The "currently configured" half preserves the existing behaviour that a source hand-removed from news-sources.json has its items dropped. Publish gate keys on _okNames.length > 0 rather than the counter because sourcesOk is now SEEDED with the deliberately-skipped healthy count (sources.length - targets.length) to keep NewsPane.qml:1123's "N of M sources unreachable" counting M over all configured sources; a counter-only gate would have let a wholly-failed partial run take the publish branch. A partial run does NOT advance fetchedAtMs — that would lie in the age readout and push the TTL out, indefinitely deferring the full refresh the healthy sources still need — but it does still writeCache(), so a heal survives a restart. NOT TOUCHED: NotifCentre.qml:721's newsRefreshButton, which already exists and whose refresh(true) already bypasses the stale gate. AN EARLIER CLAIM IN THIS FILE THAT NewsPane.qml SHIPPED NO MANUAL REFRESH WAS WRONG AND IS RETRACTED (361d99e) — it came from grepping one file when the control lives in the parent component; the operator caught it by asking "don't we already have a manual refresh?", which shrank this task from two halves to one. Gates: qmllint exit 0, new comment-stripped Python gate news-selfheal-gate 5/5 (falsified by hand before being trusted), colour-lint 150/0, motion-lint 297/0, quickshell-doctor 28/0 on re-run (a first run read 27/1, the known post-hot-reload flake). OPERATOR-VERIFIED LIVE 2026-08-19: Exercises B and C both passed. A shell restarted at 22:17:23 logged `re-attempting 1 source(s) with no cached headlines: Phoronix`, then `carried 30 item(s) forward from 3 source(s) not refreshed this run`, then `partial run (1 of 4 source(s)) — fetchedAtMs left unchanged`; news-cache.json kept fetched_at at 22:13:53 (pre-restart) with items back to 10/10/10/10, so the retry set really is derived from cache contents alone — a fresh process has no in-memory failure state — the healthy sources were carried rather than clobbered, the clock did not move, and the heal still reached disk. The no-op path logged one 'every source has headlines — nothing to re-attempt' line with zero fetches. Exercise A treated as SUBSUMED by C (same three assertions; it differs only in creating the zero-item state via a watched-file rename instead of a doctored cache plus restart). NOT exercised: the all-attempted-sources-fail partial run, which needs a deliberately unreachable host. BOUNDARY FOUND DURING VERIFICATION from the operator's log rather than by reasoning: a source that FAILS but still holds previously-cached items is NOT re-attempted, because _carryForward() preserves those items and _sourcesNeedingRetry() keys on zero cached items — observed at log lines 8508 -> 8531 -> 8532, where Hacker News failed, kept its 10 carried items, and the next open said 'every source has headlines'. Coherent with the defect this task targeted (a feed reading as DEAD, i.e. blank) but narrower than 'retry anything that failed': a stale-but-populated source still waits out the TTL. Named, not a defect. | 2026-08-19 | 9a89f51 | — |

Wrong role, not a broken binding, and the magenta fallback never firing was the proof. The shell's real colour-shifting edge language is GradientBorder.qml's primary->secondary->tertiary accent stops, used by 13 surfaces. GradientBorder itself CANNOT be mounted on this strip: it builds its ring from `_roundedRect()`, so on a bulged outline it would trace the bounding box and drop the bulge — the idiom was transplanted onto EdgeBar's own `_outlinePath` instead. One deliberate divergence: GradientBorder sweeps endpoints around a half-diagonal, which on a ~3440x16 strip compresses the spectrum into 16px at 90 degrees and pulses, so the axis is LOCKED to the long axis and scrolled, seamless via RepeatSpread with the stop list closing back on primary. A second RESERVING surface is legal because the doctor's `exclusiveZone: 0` assertion applies only to rows marked `noreserve`. And req 7's flare had to be an ADDITIVE sibling component: both panels' fills are plain `Rectangle`s that cannot render a concave corner at all, so editing GradientBorder alone would have flared the rim and left the fill square — a fix that passes every gate and looks broken. ### Pending Todos

[From .planning/todos/pending/ — ideas captured during sessions]

1. **`steamwebhelper` crash loop — reason 213 = `INVALID_INITIATOR_ORIGIN`** (`2026-08-23-steamwebhelper-crash-loop-on-nvidia-xwayland.md`, severity `minor`) — **ROOT CAUSE NAMED 2026-08-25.** Read Chromium's own `content/browser/bad_message.h` at tag `126.0.6478.126` (this build is `.183`, same branch): enum index 213 is `INVALID_INITIATOR_ORIGIN`, between `RFPH_POST_MESSAGE_INVALID_SOURCE_ORIGIN` (212) and `RFHI_BEGIN_NAVIGATION_MISSING_INITIATOR_ORIGIN` (214). CEF 126.2's own `CefFrame::LoadRequest` docs corroborate verbatim. **It is a navigation-security check with ZERO graphics involvement** — which explains why `-cef-disable-gpu` and the `htmlcache` wipe both did nothing (category errors, not deep problems), and retires the "NVIDIA + XWayland" framing along with the "test a different NVIDIA driver branch" angle. **The timing was also re-measured and the original characterisation was WRONG:** all 12/12 kills fire 3-5s after a webhelper browser start, not "minutes to hours apart" — that gap was measuring when the *user* next started the webhelper. So the old "judge any fix over hours" rule is retired in favour of a restart-count protocol (start N times, count kills within 5s), which is both cheaper and stronger. Not every start dies (40 starts / 12 kills), so it is startup-bound but not deterministic. **Not fixable from this repo** — it is Steam's own code. `--disable-site-isolation-trials` would suppress it but is NOT recommended: a real security downgrade in the process rendering the logged-in Steam store, traded against a cosmetic annoyance. No upstream Valve issue names 213; filing one is the highest-value remaining action. Workaround unchanged: `steam -shutdown`.

[Issues that affect future work]

**Open — carried into v3.0:**

1. **Phase 4 advisory review items (`04-REVIEW.md` WR-01..04)** — WR-01/02/03 now CLOSED (13-03, commit `baae579`, each fault-injection proven). **WR-04 (Logout teardown hazard) remains OPEN.** Its D-29 measurement gate was explicitly waived by the operator on 2026-07-28 to close Phase 13 — NOT PERFORMED, NOT falsified, NOT resolved. MAINT-02 stays unchecked in REQUIREMENTS.md pending WR-04's real closure. → Owned by Phase 13 (MAINT-02), partially closed.
2. **QS-03 per-screen mounting gap.** The shell root mounts one `LazyLoader` per `GlobalShortcut`, not one surface per `Quickshell.screens` entry, so an output hotplugged after startup gets no surface. Real, mechanically-verified defect — `quickshell-doctor` reports 13 passed / 1 failed on exactly this check. A `Variants`-based fix was written in 11-04 and reverted after two independent live-daemon reliability regressions on quickshell 0.3.0 (intermittent config-load race, post-hotplug visibility break). **Closed for Phase 11** via a recorded override in `11-VERIFICATION.md` (`overrides[0]`, accepted by YahiaEng 2026-07-26); **now owned by Phase 12** as its success criterion 6. Not unowned, not forgotten — but still an open defect until Phase 12 closes it.
3. **Two Phase 11 backstop truths accepted as UNVERIFIED (2026-07-26).** Recorded as `overrides[1]`/`overrides[2]` in `11-VERIFICATION.md` and carried in `behavior_unverified_items`. Neither is evidence of correct behaviour — both are known unknowns:
   - *XF86 duplicate-key handler determinism* — never exercised; testing it means deliberately breaking a working keybind config and restarting the session twice. Relevant to Phases 14 and 16, which each add a global keybind. Re-open if a bind-resolution conflict ever surfaces.
   - *Zero-output survival* — physically untestable on this single-monitor host. Phase 12 needs multi-output test infrastructure for QS-03 anyway; that is the natural place to finally exercise it.

4. **Phase 15 closed with acknowledged gaps (2026-08-02, operator direction).** `15-VERIFICATION.md` reads `status: passed` because UAT round 2 exercised exactly its three `behavior_unverified_items` against real hardware — but the verifier was never re-run over plans 15-10..15-14 or commits `12575ac`/`18da48c`, and **no security review exists** (`/gsd-secure-phase 15` never ran) despite the phase touching wifi passphrase handling, an autostart override that removes the system's NetworkManager secret agent, and a new `nmcli` subprocess. Full list in that file's `## Acknowledged Gaps`. → Not owned by any phase; pick up deliberately.
5. **G-15-9 open and unmeasured.** A suspected D-Bus reply timeout may render a successful-but-slow bluetooth pair as "Couldn't pair". Rests on one log line plus one user account; the measurement (pair a fresh peer, wait 30-40s before confirming) was never performed. → `15-UAT.md`.
6. **The notification-server replacement MUST declare `body` and `actions` capability.** blueman picks its presentation by querying the notification daemon; a server missing either capability silently demotes bluetooth pairing to a raw GTK dialog that sits *behind* the layer-shell panel — reintroducing the G-15-4 failure this milestone spent a plan removing for wifi, and presenting as a bluetooth bug rather than a notification bug. `Quickshell.Services.Notifications` exposes `actionsSupported` for exactly this. → Owned by whichever phase replaces swaync; recorded in `15-audio-connectivity-panels/deferred-items.md`.
7. **`hypr-equivalence-check` binds.json baseline drift** — pre-existing since 15-02's Super+A keybind; needs the surgical one-record baseline insertion 14-10 used, not a wholesale re-snapshot. → `15-audio-connectivity-panels/deferred-items.md`.

WORKED 2026-08-25 (late) — **260825-t6j: revealed solidly, and the bulge is its panel's width now.** `2ea71b9f`.

**BOTH FIXES CAME OUT OF TWO LOG LINES.** No screenshots: the display sleeps into `FALLBACK` where layer surfaces render black, and `grim` cannot be raced against a 500ms animation at ~150ms per capture. `popout: ... panel=360x281 rootInset=8.0 margins=[t 838.0, r 8.0]` and `barbulge: span=865..1140` carried every number needed — panel 281 along-axis at screen 862..1143, bulge 275, shortfall exactly 6.

**THE SPAWN GEOMETRY WAS ALREADY RIGHT; THE FADE WAS THE DEFECT.** The closed panel already sits one full `panelWidth` right of open, entirely outside a surface whose right edge is pinned to the slab's flat face at 2502 — wholly hidden, boundary cutting it as it travels. That is a drawer. What contradicted it was `spawnClip.opacity: opened ? 1 : 0` running across the whole slide, so a solid object emerging from behind a solid bar was translucent the entire way out. **An edge reveal and an opacity ramp are two transitions doing the same job**, and the second is what reads as "appearing beside the bar" rather than "extending from behind it". Opacity is 1 while attached now. Unattached keeps the fade, and that split is principled: that path has no occluder at all, so the fade is the only thing easing it in — which is also exactly why `Dashboard.qml` fades, sliding from off-screen with nothing behind it to be revealed from.

**TWO THINGS RECORDED AS IMPOSSIBLE, because both look like the obvious fix.** (a) The bar cannot occlude the popout: popout is overlay (3), bar is top (2), and demoting the popout to 2 does not help because within a level wlroots stacks by CREATION ORDER and the popout is built per summon — it would still land above a bar that has existed since shell start, while anything lower puts it under application windows. (b) The surface cannot reach behind the bar's body: `Bar.qml:613` had already measured that margins subtract from the USABLE area, not the screen edge, so `ExclusionMode.Ignore` does not opt out of the bar's own 50px reservation — `margins.right: 72` landed the edge at 2438 (2510-72), not 2488.

**THE BULGE'S 6px WAS A TRADE, AND THE TRADE WAS THE BUG.** `buildOutline` draws each concave shoulder OUTSIDE the span it is handed — the flat run travels to `xr + f` before filleting back to `xr` — so round 4 inset the span by `f` at each end to stop the bulge overshooting the panel and showing through the flare band as a bar-coloured tab. That made the total FOOTPRINT match the panel (275 + 2x3 = 281) while the visible FACE stayed short, which is what the operator saw. **Fixed by removing the cause instead of paying for it:** `f: 0` on that one `buildOutline` call, the only call that differs — rails and the static centre bulge keep the token. With no shoulder nothing is drawn outside the span, so the face is the panel's exact extent AND nothing overshoots. The concave blend is not lost: the two `AttachedCorner` flares already weld those corners, and a clean butt at the panel's edge is the joint their quarter-pipes are built to sweep into. **Checked in the solver rather than assumed** — `_arcCentre` is pure midpoint arithmetic with no division, coincident endpoints match the expected centre exactly, `A 0 0` with identical start/end is omitted per the SVG arc spec, and the `fillet + corner <= extra` invariant holds at 0 + 1 <= 14. The round-4 comment arguing for the inset was rewritten rather than left contradicting the code above it, and now states that `f` and the inset are ONE decision.

**OUTSTANDING, OPERATOR:** open a popout and read `barbulge:` — it should now say `span=862..1143` (281) against `865..1140` (275). Watch for the bar-coloured tab returning beside a flare's arc; if it does, `f: 0` did not fully remove the outside-the-span drawing and the named fallback is restoring the inset, which trades the width back. `quickshell-doctor` still unrun and still operator-only.

WORKED 2026-08-25 (late) — **260825-sgm: the popouts slide out of the bulge now, and the reveal I invented is gone.** `1ef21341`, `3469c7d1`.

**THE ROUND-4 REVEAL WAS MINE AND ITS REASON DOES NOT SURVIVE THE REFERENCE.** I had replaced a translate with a growing-width container because, as a translate, "the flares ride along so the weld only arrives in the final frame." The dashboard's own `AttachedCorner` pair rides its panel and lands with it — the reference has the identical property, and the operator holds the reference up as correct. **A fix invented for a problem the reference also has is not a fix, it is a divergence.** Before designing around something, check whether the surface being copied already does it.

**THE CORRECTION IS SMALL BECAUSE THE SHAPE WAS ALREADY RIGHT.** `spawnClip` stays but is stripped to the OPACITY CARRIER — `popoutBackground`, `popoutBorderClip`, both flares and `content` are SIBLINGS of `panel` anchored to it, not children, so they do not inherit `panel`'s opacity and that container is their only common parent. `Dashboard.qml`'s `y: opened ? 0 : -height` then runs on `x`, rotated onto the right slab. Not anchored (an `anchors.right` OVERRIDES `x` and the slide would be inert — and it bought nothing, since the surface's `implicitWidth` IS `panelWidth`, so `x: 0` already seats it flush). Not transformed (flares are siblings anchored to `panel`'s edges; a `Translate` would strand them at the bulge — `Launcher.qml` may use one only because its background FILLS its panel).

**DISTANCE IS THE PANEL'S OWN WIDTH, NEVER THE SURFACE'S** — `Launcher.qml:612` states it outright, earned when staged layer-surface configures dragged its panel long after it had opened.

**THE CONSTRUCTION-TIME TRAP IS GUARDED NOW, NOT DODGED.** It has bitten this file twice and both times on the CLOSED value: round 3 read `triggerCentre`/`vertical` and stacked all eight popouts at the top of the bar; round 4 read `_slideFromBar` for a closed width and ran 360 → 0 → 360. Round 4's answer was to make the closed value unconditional — that works, but it forbids the closed state depending on posture at all, which here it must (attached slides on `x`, unattached drops on `y`). `Launcher.qml`'s arm guard adopted whole: while disarmed the offsets SNAP instead of animating, so the panel waits off-view at whatever they resolve to. **One half is not in the launcher and had to be added:** the launcher branches direction on a shell-root property, this file's comes from the loader, so `on_SlideFromBarChanged` restarts the settle timer too — otherwise a late `vertical` lands after the arm and animates the wrong axis.

**SCOPE CORRECTION, CAUGHT BEFORE ANY CODE.** I offered the operator a choice between two families and described the config panels wrong. `PanelDialog` is `anchors.top: true` with `edge: "top"` flares — welded to the TOP rail's bulge exactly like the dashboard, already the close copy, nothing to do. The anchor sat four lines from the weld code I had just read; I inferred the edge from "the bar is on the right" instead of reading it.

**QUICKSHELL-DOCTOR KILLED THE SESSION** — exit 137, Hyprland down, the second loss today. **It is not a static gate:** line 1590 runs `systemctl --user restart quickshell.service`, and it summons live surfaces and takes a `grim` capture. The standing never-restart rule already covered this TRANSITIVELY and I missed the indirection — a wrapper inherits the hazard of everything it calls. Grep an unfamiliar gate for `systemctl`/`grim`/`qs ipc call`/`hyprctl dispatch` first. The other five gates are pure file readers and are safe.

**OUTSTANDING, OPERATOR ONLY:** `quickshell-doctor` (28 checks) unrun against this change, and the motion unseen — the output is `FALLBACK`, so layer surfaces render black. A popout should EMERGE LEFTWARD out of the bulge rather than grow, and retract on the mirrored curve with a brief recoil at the start of the exit; that recoil is the entrance overshoot played backwards, not a defect.

WORKED 2026-08-25 (late night) — **260825-pyf round 4: the flare tab removed, and the popouts now EXTRUDE from the bar's rim.** `ad8920e4`, pushed.

**THE FLARE TAB WAS THE BULGE SHOWING THROUGH — and the mechanism generalises.** `buildOutline` draws each concave shoulder OUTSIDE the span it is handed: from the flat run it travels to `xr + f` before filleting back to `xr`. So a bulge spanning exactly the panel's extent actually reaches `f` PAST the panel at both ends. The panel hides the bulge wherever it overlaps it — but in the flare band beyond the panel's edge only the flare's quarter-pipe fill is painted, and that fill HUGS THE WELD CORNER, leaving the outer part transparent. The bulge showed through there as a small square tab of bar-coloured pixels. **Diagnosed by a CONTROLLED COMPARISON rather than by reading the code:** with the bulge forced to zero depth the same rows read 132-139 (background) where they had read ~606 (the bar's rim blue), and nothing else in the frame changed. Span is now inset by the fillet radius — the stated condition (`xl - f >= panelTop`, `xr + f <= panelBottom`), not a padding guess.

**THE SPAWN IS A REVEAL NOW, NOT A TRANSLATE.** Measured at 8x slow motion, the old motion swept its left rim 2235 -> 2188 -> 2166 -> 2153: a fully-formed card travelling its own 360px width, with the flares riding along so the weld only arrived in the final frame. A container pinned to the surface's right edge — the bar's own coloured rim — now grows its width from zero, with the panel anchored to that same edge so it NEVER MOVES. The flares sit at the panel's right end, so they are inside the reveal from the first frame and the weld is present the whole way out.

**ALL SIX VISUAL CHILDREN HAD TO MOVE TOGETHER**, and this is the structural fact to remember about this file: they anchor to EACH OTHER (`anchors.fill: panel`, the flares' `panel.top`/`panel.bottom`), and QML permits anchoring only to a sibling or the parent — so leaving any of them outside the new container would have made those anchors illegal. A first attempt to wrap a contiguous LINE RANGE was caught by an assertion before it wrote: root-level `readonly property` declarations are interleaved between those children and would have been swallowed into a child Item.

**THE SAME CONSTRUCTION-VS-CONFIGURATION TRAP BIT AGAIN, IN A NEW PLACE.** The reveal's width first read `(opened || !_slideFromBar) ? width : 0`. `_slideFromBar` is `attached && vertical`, and **`vertical` is assigned by the loader AFTER construction** — so at construction this evaluated to the FULL width, and by the time `vertical` arrived and flipped it, the deferred `opened` had already fired. Width went 360 -> 0 -> 360 and the panel just appeared. Measured: 0.9s into an 8x-slowed reveal, content still began at x=2142, the panel's full left edge. **The lesson beyond the fix: depending on a loader-assigned property for the CLOSED/initial state is what makes this dangerous, because the initial state is the one evaluated at construction.** Unconditional 0 is safe — an unclipped Item of zero width still renders its children, which covers the unattached case.

**TWO INSTRUMENT LESSONS:** (a) racing `grim` against an animation does not work — ~150ms per capture against a 500ms motion, and even at 8x my frames kept landing outside the window. The shell's own `console.log` is the reliable channel for anything time-varying, and it is what confirmed the reveal (`w=8 -> 15 -> 22 -> ... -> 357, clip=true`). (b) **The bulge log proved the bulge had NEVER painted by emitting NOTHING** — an absent signal, readable only because the line logs on change. It fired every animated frame though (2042 lines in one session) and is now throttled to the two states that carry information.

**A SHELL GOTCHA THAT WASTED TWO STEPS:** `grep` output was silently swallowed several times in this environment — `grep -c pattern file` printed nothing while the pattern demonstrably matched. Reading the log through Python (`re.sub` the ANSI codes, then filter) is reliable; bare `grep` on `~/.cache/quickshell.log` is not.

**GATES once each:** doctor 28/0, colour-lint 365/0, motion-lint 552/0, settings-index 121/0, keybind-doctor 13/0.

WORKED 2026-08-25 (night) — **260825-pyf round 3. Three operator-reported faults, all mine, all now MEASURED ON SCREEN.** `b4173689`, pushed.

**THE PROCESS FAILURE FIRST, because it caused the rest.** The operator's standing rule is to verify with screenshots and pixels. I measured COORDINATES and cropped a 60px corner, and never once looked at a whole panel or at a popout at all. Every fault below was visible in a single screenshot I did not take. Coordinates agreeing with a formula is not the same as the surface looking right.

**FAULT 1 — flares alongside the old corners, and the attached edge still drawing its rim. THE FIX ALREADY EXISTED IN THIS TREE AND I DID NOT APPLY IT.** `Dashboard.qml`'s `dashboardBorderClip` (operator round 3 of 260823-9ak) describes this exact symptom in its own comment: *"the panel's own side border runs straight past the flare's arc as a second parallel rim line, which is what 'the concave flares exist alongside the old geometry' described."* Once a flare welds a corner, the attached edge AND the adjacent `attachedCornerRadius` of both neighbouring sides stop being outer edges of the merged silhouette, so the ring must not draw them. Applied now to BOTH `SectionPopout` and `PanelDialog`. **The asymmetry is load-bearing:** insetting the TOP moves the clip's origin down, so PanelDialog's ring needs `y: -flareRadius`; insetting only the RIGHT does not move the origin, so the popout's needs none.

**FAULT 2 — the popout spawned far from the bar, TWO independent causes plus a third found by the same measurement.** (a) It targeted the BULGE's face, but the bulge is not a shelf beside the panel — it protrudes OVER it, exactly as the top rail's bulge covers the dashboard's top 14px. (b) **`margins.right` is measured from the USABLE area, not the screen edge, despite `exclusionMode: ExclusionMode.Ignore` and this frame's own comment claiming otherwise.** Measured: margins.right 72 gave a right edge of 2438; 2560−72 = 2488 but 2510−72 = 2438. That 50px IS the reported gap. (c) The usable boundary uses the TOTAL reservation (margins 6 + exclusiveZone 44 = 50), not `reservedZoneExtent` alone — that cost a further 6px. Right edge now measures **2502**, the slab's flat face, exactly.

**FAULT 3 — the anchor was published only on the POINTER paths.** `publishAnchor()` was called from the hover and click handlers only, so a popout summoned any other way arrived with `_publishedCentre` still 0. Logged precisely: `triggerCentre=0 rootCentre=164`. Now published from the loader's own activation — **the one place every summon path passes through**, so it is correct by construction rather than by remembering to add a call to each new caller.

**AND THE BULGE HAD NEVER PAINTED ONCE.** `Binding { target: barWindow; property: "popoutBulgeDepth" }` — a `Binding` aimed at its own file's ROOT OBJECT — silently never applied, while the three `Binding`s aimed at `PopoutController` in that same file worked fine. A direct binding expression animates through the `Behavior` identically. **The diagnostic is what found it: NO log line at all rather than a wrong value** — an absent signal, which is only readable because the log prints on change.

**A FINDING I RECORDED AND HAD TO RETRACT:** I claimed Quickshell's IPC surface is fixed at process start and hot reload never registers a new `IpcHandler`, and told the operator a restart was needed. **Wrong.** The `popout` target appeared later in the same session with the shell never restarted (launch time unchanged, 2h44m). Registration LAGS the reload, and during that window a probe function on an already-registered target was missing too — which is why the positive control agreed with the wrong conclusion. A control rules out "something about my new code"; it cannot rule out a timing window, because both arms can sit inside the same one. Memory corrected.

**VERIFIED ON SCREEN this time:** the wifi panel's top-left corner captured at 6x BESIDE the dashboard's own at the same zoom — same flare, no top rim, no square corner. The popout captured whole — rim on left/top/bottom, curving outward into the bar at both right corners, none on the bar-facing edge, seated on the slab. Bulge animation confirmed in the log reaching depth 14 over span 992..1268.

WORKED 2026-08-25 (late) — **260825-pyf round 2: the popout bug I shipped is fixed, and the three config panels now spawn from the bulge too.** `047740db`, pushed.

**I SHIPPED A BUG AND THE OPERATOR CAUGHT IT:** *"All popouts spawn from the same location which is at the top and far away from the bar."* Exactly right. `SectionPopout` published its bulge root from `Component.onCompleted`, but **`PopoutTrigger.qml:173-175` assigns `vertical` and `triggerCentre` onto the item AFTER creating it** — so at construction both were still at their declared defaults, every popout published a centre of 0, and the bar clamped that to one fixed position.

**THE REASONING ERROR IS THE LESSON, not the line.** I reasoned that a fresh-per-summon component makes construction time and summon time the same instant. That is TRUE — and it is not the point. The loader's property assignments land AFTER construction. **Being BUILT at summon time is not being CONFIGURED at construction time.** Bindings now, which does not reintroduce the hazard the snapshot existed to avoid: the snapshot lives upstream in `PopoutTrigger._publishedCentre`, and forwarding a snapshot is not re-measuring it. `openExtent` had a SECOND independent reason to be late — the popout is content-bounded, so its size is 0 until the body lays out.

**THE CONFIG PANELS (audio/wifi/bluetooth) NOW WELD TO THE TOP RAIL** like the dashboard: `margins.top` 0, `AttachedCorner` flares, the dashboard's slide-and-fade with the MIRRORED dismissal, and their layer rules moved `slide` -> `fade` because a compositor slide on top of a QML slide is two motions on one surface. The rail's bulge follows whichever surface is open and is sized off `PanelDialog.panelWidth` — the panels are 850 against the dashboard's 760, so the fixed bulge left them overhanging their own root by 45px a side.

**MEASURED, per the operator's standing rule:** the wifi surface moved from `830,16 850x620` to `806,6 898x620` — welded (y 16 -> 6), widened by exactly 2x24 for the flares, panel still centred at 830. Flare confirmed by raw pixel dump WITH A POSITIVE CONTROL (row y=3, pure wallpaper, luminance 538): the gradient rim sits at x=813 on row y=6, 821 at y=10, 825 at y=14, reaching the panel's own edge by y=18 — a concave arc at full 24px against the rail, tapering to nothing. **An earlier binary threshold clipped the arc's anti-aliased tip and read 13px; the raw dump corrected it — a reminder that a threshold is an instrument and can be wrong.** Dashboard regression check: its own y=6 transitions are byte-identical before and after (854, 864/865, 1645/1646, 1656).

**THE EXIT WAS NOT ACTUALLY PLAYING, and only measurement showed it.** `openPanel` closes via `closeAllPanels()`, which set `active = false` directly and destroyed the surface on the FIRST FRAME of its exit — measured 112ms against a 500ms spatial-in token. That was the MAIN path (Super+A, the tile chevrons, the IPC verb); only Esc and focus-loss reached `requestDismiss()`, so the animation would have looked broken exactly where it is used most and correct where it is used least. Now 576ms.

**CLOSING IS SPLIT, and a GATE found the reason rather than my eye.** A genuine dismiss animates; a close that is REPLACING one panel with another is instant. `quickshell-doctor` reported `cross[count=2]`: all three panels are the same 850px frame in the same centred position, so an animated overlap reads as a glitch rather than a crossfade, and it broke D-15-25's at-most-one-panel invariant.

**THE DOCTOR'S SETTLE WENT 0.3s -> 1.0s, and that is a widening, NOT a weakening.** The invariant is still zero panels once nothing is open, and a panel that never closes is still caught, because 1.0s is far past any exit the motion tokens can produce. Recorded explicitly because "I relaxed the gate that failed" is the shape of a bad fix, and this is not one.

**GATES once each:** doctor 28/0 (re-run only after the one real failure it caught), colour-lint 365/0, motion-lint 552/0, settings-index 121/0, keybind-doctor 13/0, hypr-equivalence 3/0. `reserved` still `[0,6,50,6]`.

**STILL NOT PIXEL-VERIFIED: the attached POPOUT.** The fix is code-evident but has never been rendered, and after shipping one bug here it should not be called done on reasoning alone. Bar popouts cannot be summoned from the agent shell (no IPC until restart, `hl.dsp.movecursor` nil, `wtype` types into the focused window).

WORKED 2026-08-25 (evening) — **quick task 260825-pyf: the bar's eight glance surfaces are on the edge-bar language.** `f1e0cc3f`..`e7b77e24`. **ONE OPERATOR CLICK IS OUTSTANDING** — see the end.

**ALL EIGHT POPOUTS ARE ONE FRAME** (`modules/bar/SectionPopout.qml`; the eight `*Popout.qml` files are bodies declared into it), so this was one frame change plus wiring, not eight ports. Worth knowing before scoping anything else in that family.

**THE BULGE NEEDED NO NEW GEOMETRY, and this is the reusable part.** The bar's slab is a vertical run with pill caps and a bulge on its inner face — precisely what `edgebarpath.js`'s `buildOutline` already draws. Passing `flip: true` (the slab's OUTER edge is its right side) and `axis: "vertical"` gets the rails' own fillet radii, the 123-assertion golden test's coverage and every sweep flag derived rather than hand-authored. Verified BEFORE adopting rather than after: with `bulge: false` it reproduces `_weldRoundedRect(52, 1440, 26)` exactly — same bbox, same shape, differing only in drawing each cap as two quarter arcs (which is what keeps every arc inside `_arcCentre`'s quarter-circle domain). Measured after the swap: slab rims still 2502–2505 and 2550–2553.

**ATTACHED IS CONTINUOUS-ONLY AS A CONSEQUENCE, NOT A CHOICE.** Outside Continuous the bar paints no edge at all — `barContent` is a bare `Item`, and the slab and its core are both `visible: _continuousWeld`. So there is nothing to swell, and segmented/halo/brackets/off all take the unattached posture. Only `off` was named in the request; the other three follow from the same fact and are recorded rather than silently assumed.

**FINDING 1 — QUICKSHELL'S IPC SURFACE IS FIXED AT PROCESS START.** Hot reload does NOT re-register `IpcHandler` targets or functions. Proven with a POSITIVE CONTROL instead of inferred from one failure: a probe function added to the *already registered* `bar` target also failed to appear, which rules out anything about the new handler. So the new `popout` target is correct code that is inert until the next restart. Without the control this would have read as a bug in the handler and been "fixed" repeatedly.

**FINDING 2 — `reserved` IS `[left, top, right, bottom]`.** The first probe assumed `[left, right, top, bottom]`, CONFIRMED ITSELF on the left edge, and reported a 34px mismatch on the top. Checking all four edges at once settled it in one step: window edge = `reserved` + `gaps_out` holds exactly on every edge (0+20=20, 6+20=26, 2560−50−20=2490, 1440−6−20=1414). **A one-edge check would have shipped the wrong index order.**

**FINDING 3 — A PRE-EXISTING DEFECT, FOUND BY MEASURING.** `SectionPopout.qml` wrote its own fuse: *"It matches gaps_out by value rather than by binding — if general:gaps_out changes, this alignment needs revisiting (a QML surface cannot read it live)."* It changed. `gaps_out` is 20 now (operator-confirmed intended), `border_size` 0, clients at `[20,26]`. **FOUR surfaces align to the window edge and all four are wrong:** `SectionPopout` 10, `Dashboard.qml:330` `drawerTopMargin` 10, `launcher/Launcher.qml:159` `drawerTopMargin` 10, `centre/NotifCentre.qml:194` 13 — against a true 20. New singleton `modules/WindowInset.qml` reads it live and re-reads on every `configreloaded` (which is exactly when it changes: `lib/reload.sh` runs `hyprctl reload` on every theme and motion apply, re-applying `overrides.lua` where gaps_out lives). **ONLY SectionPopout IS WIRED** — the other three are a real defect but not what was asked, and moving three panels 10px unasked is the operator's call. Each is a one-line change to `WindowInset.insetFor("top")`.

**TWO PARSING SHAPES, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED:** `gaps_out` returns `{"css": "20 20 20 20"}` with **no `int` key at all**; `border_size` returns `{"int": 0}`. The first draft guessed a `custom` key with an `int` fallback and BOTH were wrong. `border_size` is exposed but deliberately NOT folded into `inset`: at border 0 this host cannot distinguish the two definitions of a window's edge, and `NotifCentre.qml:194` and `SectionPopout.qml:186` **disagree** about whether Hyprland's `at` includes the border. Picking a side on an untestable host is exactly how the hardcoded 13 got there.

**A NAMING TRAP THAT WILL LOOK LIKE A BUG:** the weld flares use `edge: "bottom"` on the flare at the panel's **TOP**. `AttachedCorner` names its two lines for the horizontal case it was written for, and here those roles are SWAPPED (the attached edge is vertical). The shape depends only on WHICH CORNER the material hugs, and `(edge, side)` together pick it. "Correcting" the name to match would put the arc on the wrong diagonal and render a CONVEX lump — the failure that file's own header warns "will look deliberate and be wrong".

**THE PANEL IS POSITIONED, NOT TRANSFORMED**, because the two flares are SIBLINGS anchored to its edges: anchors track real geometry, so moving `panel.x` carries them, while a Translate would have slid the panel out from under two flares left standing at the bulge. `Dashboard.qml`'s own panel/flare pair is built this way for this reason.

**GATES, once each:** doctor 28/0, colour-lint 365/0, motion-lint 552/0, settings-index 121/0, keybind-doctor 13/0. Counts ROSE with the new files; none fell. `reserved` still `[0,6,50,6]`.

**WHAT IS NOT VERIFIED, and why it is a host limit rather than a shortcut: THE ATTACHED POPOUT HAS NEVER BEEN RENDERED.** Bar popouts were the only summonable surfaces in this shell with no IPC target; `hl.dsp.movecursor` is nil on this build and `wtype` types into the focused window, so there is no way to put one on screen from the agent shell. The `popout` IPC target fixes this permanently but needs a restart to register, and restarting quickshell from a tool call kills the session (standing rule, broken three times).

Resume file: None. NEXT ACTION: operator clicks any bar capsule to pin a popout and leaves it open, then the attached geometry can be measured. Also awaiting a decision on the three unfixed `gaps_out` consumers.
